## Summary

Suit & Equipment is the player's body and kit as simulation: the per-player record of how much air, warmth, suit integrity and lamp charge a crew member has left, which tools, glowsticks and carried modules they hold, which of the four beam shapes is on their chest, and the rules that turn the true state of the room around them into the cues they can hear, see and feel on their own avatar — and, when those cues run out, into the player's own death. Read this GDD if you build or design anything that asks "can this person breathe, move, see or carry": the Controller (which reads one value, `locomotion_cap`), the Emitter State (which asks whether the lamp may be on), Interaction (whose verbs spend Suit's counts), Attention, Sanity, Boarding & Salvage and the HUD & Suit Layer. It fixes one block (`suit.state`), one hook (`suit.vitals`), one command (`equip_tool`), a published mutator and read-view set, the three-axis vitals ladder with its tell floor, and the closed set of body cues — and it never draws a pixel, plays a sound, or reads Sanity or Attention.

**Two registers, kept apart (SE-25, SE-29):** a **body cue** (breath, visor fog, rime, shiver, seam jet, valve click, stagger, walk-only, the load signature) is a pure function of the occupant's compartment band enums and `suit.state` and can never be faked, suppressed or tinted; a **reading** (the O2 numeral, the reserve, integrity, warmth, charge) reaches the HUD only through `true_value(key, player_id)`, is architected lie-capable, and is left honest in the MVP.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` (systems-index Systems Enumeration row 10 / Recommended Design Order 11; Category Gameplay) · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer`, `Life-Support Graph`, `Item & Module Database`, `Player Controller & Input`, `Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query`, `Save/Load & Versioned Format` (all designed); Ship Section & Layout, Interaction and 2D Lighting & Occlusion (designed 2026-10-26, 18:38-19:34; contract current; Interaction 19:34; re-verified at the final advisor review, 2026-10-27), Ship Body & Interior Physics (designed; checked at the consistency pass, 2026-10-27). Depended on by 5 systems named by the index (Boarding & Salvage, Attention, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, HUD & Suit Layer, and Failure Contract & Re-wake at Vertical Slice) plus Interaction, whose index edge is missing and routed (Cross-References). MVP slice: Overview.

## Overview

Suit & Equipment is the simulation of the player's body and what it carries. In one sentence: it is a scene-tree-free, integer-only `SIM` block, `suit.state`, one record per bound seat keyed by PlayerId, advanced by one divisor-6 hook, `suit.vitals`, that holds the seat's breathing reserve, lamp cell, thermal load, suit integrity, three vitals rungs, beam shape, hand-rail tool, counted stowed stock and single carried module — and derives from it, on read, the one value the Controller obeys (`locomotion_cap`: FULL, WALK_ONLY or NONE) and the closed set of body cues that every presenter, audio system and crewmate conforms to. It is written only through one command (`equip_tool`) and a small set of published Class B mutators, reads the Life-Support Graph's true band enums for the occupant's compartment, draws air from the room through the Graph's `draw_air` mutator, and calls the Emitter State's `bind_beam` and `force_off`. It owns the answer to "what happens to a person in bad air, cold, heat or a damaged suit", including the answer the Graph explicitly hands to it: player death.

The player never operates this system as a screen. They meet it as **a body with a few honest signs**: their own breath in the dark, a click at the collar when the reserve valve takes over, a fogging visor, rime creeping over the shoulder plates, a shiver at rest, a stumble and then a walk where there was a run, the slow deep exhale and the creak of a pack that now carries the 30 kg hull plate, and a lamp on the chest that costs something to keep lit. Beside those, on the HUD's thin reading register, five readings — the suit's O2 numeral (the suit-sensed `p_o2` of the occupant's compartment, not a Graph overlay), the reserve, integrity, warmth, and charge — say the same things in numbers. The two are never the same channel: the body cues are on the Graph's closed consequence channel and cannot be removed, added or tinted by any hallucination; the readings reach the HUD only through the Filter's `perceived`, which wraps `true_value(key, player_id)`, are architected lie-capable (so Sanity can one day make a numeral glide), and are left honest in the MVP. A first-time player learns to trust the breath before the number, and that ordering is the point.

The block exists because several decisions made elsewhere need one honest owner. The Graph guarantees that the *cause* of a death — the room's band, a module's failure — was warned, then states that "Player death is Suit's rule" (LS-13) and leaves respiration, insulation, the O2 source and the cue effect to this GDD (its Open Question 5 asks whether the suit carries a tank). The Controller reads exactly one Suit value and expects the ladder behind it to be defined here (its Open Question 9). The Item Database makes the carried-weight body cue *binding* on Suit for stowed mass as well as the carried module. The Emitter State leaves the beam's charge, drain and `CHARGE_EMPTY` to Suit and asks that Suit's text and its own agree. Save/Load expects a `PER_PLAYER` × `WORLD` block that rolls back with the death stub. The result is a quiet bottleneck: the index lists five dependents (Boarding & Salvage, Attention, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, HUD & Suit Layer, Failure Contract & Re-wake) and Interaction's verbs call Suit's mutators in practice.

**What the game loses without it.** There is no consequence for standing in bad air, no cold, no heat, and therefore no body for the Graph's clock structure to land on; there is no finite lamp, so "go dark" has only Attention's reason and no physical one; there are no tool slots for the cutter and welder that the MVP's salvage and weld require; there is no honest field check for carried and stowed weight (the sprite is module-body and may lie, the breath may not), so phantom salvage would have no cheap falsifier; and there is no second opinion beside the instruments, so the concept's fourth pillar — "no lie is unfalsifiable" — would have nothing to point at on the player's own avatar. The concept's single hypothesis (a 30-minute boarding-then-breach loop over a visible life-support graph is tense and absorbing) needs the player to be a body the atmosphere can hurt.

**Index wording, reconciled.** The index (rationale bullet 10) describes "suit vitals (O2, integrity, local temperature and pressure read from the Graph's compartment atmosphere) as a PER_PLAYER block keyed by PlayerId, loadout and tool slots, the chest-mounted lamp (hands-free; the four beam shapes) and deployables — including throwable glowsticks — as emitters, carried modules", and says each tool's effect belongs to the system it acts on. This GDD honours that split exactly: it owns slots, counts, charge, vitals and the cue set; the tools' effects are Interaction's and Assembly's (cutter, welder), Sanity's (scanner) and Attention's and Horror's (suppressant); the beam record, shape table and exposure maths are the Emitter State's, with Suit supplying only the shape choice (`bind_beam`), the charge and the force-off; glowsticks as deployable emitters are created by Interaction's throw through the Emitter State, with Suit supplying the count. Two wordings are refreshed rather than followed: the kernel's Interactions row 4 says Suit "pushes noise/power stimuli to Attention", but Suit pushes none in the MVP (verb owners push, SE-31, routed); and the index and the Controller/Save/Load drafts name the block `vitals.state`, which is replaced by `suit.state` (SE-1, a consistency-pass sweep). Numbering, once and for all: this system is **Systems Enumeration row 10** and **Recommended Design Order 11**; enumeration row 11 is Ship Section & Layout Data Model, a different system.

**The MVP slice and what waits.** **MVP (this GDD, playable solo):** `suit.state` and `suit.vitals`; the one command `equip_tool`; room-air breathing through `draw_air` with the auto-engaging reserve (`RESERVE_S`) and the explicit top-up - V7 `top_up` as Interaction's TAP **if the Graph amends LS-26**, otherwise `TOPUP_MODE` PASSIVE with `CELL_ENABLED` 0 (OQ-2, a **USER DECISION** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*); the finite lamp cell with `CELL_WARN_S` and `force_off`; the fixed four-tool roster (slots 1-4), the suppressant with 3 charges and no refill (**USER DECISION, OQ-34: the creative-director recommends removing it from the MVP kit; slot 4 stands until the user rules**), one glowstick stack of 4 starting at `GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT`; the three vitals axes, four rungs, the rung limiter and the `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S` floor; the MVP cue set of SE-27 (breath, vapour, visor fog, rime, shiver, valve click, load layer, seam jet, HEAVY back-frame, stagger, walk-only); the external `stagger` submission on a two-band pressure drop inside `STAGGER_WINDOW_TICKS` (SE-35, evaluated by D.12; the Controller's registered `SYSTEM` type, OQ-11 answered); the five readings through `true_value`, honest; death = cap NONE, the Controller window, restart from the last autosave. **Specified but data-dormant at MVP:** SCORCH burn and arc damage (no guaranteed SCORCH room), the self-patch (Interaction IA-17 hosts it), the exogenous harm path (friendly harm is VS-B; Horror's STRIKE lands through it, HE-15), `revive` (declared, no effect). **Vertical Slice A:** reading corruption through the Perception Filter (OQ-15), the phantom-salvage field check and its blind-audio precondition (AC-SE-42), a hook-offset stagger if the Graph takes one. **Vertical Slice B:** `revive` (OQ-18), the `SHARED_SUMMARY` amendment (OQ-5), four-seat I-SUPPLY (OQ-14), friendly harm live, crewmate overlays. **Alpha and later:** scars and a persistent patch overlay (OQ-21), slots 5-6, any heat-side insulation shift. Nothing in this list adds a progression, an inventory screen or a weapon.

*Implementation questions raised while designing and deferred to ADRs (→ `/architecture-decision`): the per-seat layout of the block (a dictionary of small dictionaries keyed by PlayerId versus packed arrays, given the 4.7 rule that setting one packed-array element no longer calls the property setter, so dirty keys are marked in the mutators) (ADR candidate 1); the Suit data-file convention shared with the Item Database (integers-only JSON versus a `Resource`) (ADR candidate 2); the stowed-stock model (one counted stack per `def_id`) (ADR candidate 3); the integer-division convention for negative operands in the thermal integrator (ADR candidate 4). No ADR exists yet (`docs/architecture/` holds only `tr-registry.yaml`). The pinned engine reference (`docs/engine-reference/godot/`) has no `scripting.md` or `core.md`; GDScript integer-math cost and `RefCounted` block performance are NOT SOURCEABLE from it, and every cost number here is a model parameter for the spike.*

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: direct, with a cost layer.** The player experiences this system directly: it is their body, their light and their hands. *(creative-director consulted; three framings were weighed and logged. Framing A "The Body Is the Last Instrument" is the headline. Framing C "Two Clocks" — the reserve and the cell as countdowns — is adopted as its price, not its headline: a bare clock makes the numeral the fantasy, and the numeral is the one surface architected to lie. Framing B "The Kit" — loadout mastery — was rejected: it breeds an inventory screen and a weight readout, invites per-item insulation and cell upgrades that the Emitter State's "no light progression" and the anti-pillar "not a freeform builder" forbid, and turns the hand rail into a menu. The kit is fixed by design; mastery is in using it.)*

> You are a body in a suit, and you have two instruments: the light on your chest and the air in your lungs. The suit lets you go where people cannot, and every second there is borrowed.
>
> You learn it the way you learn to read a pipe. The air thins and your breath comes shallow and quick before any number falls. A click at your collar and the breath turns close and dry: the room is no longer what you breathe, your back is. Rime creeps over your shoulder plates and you start to shiver while the panel still shows its first zone. The plate you stowed is on your back and the breath goes slow and deep, with a creak under it. Your lamp is on your chest, and you know without looking how much of it you have left, because a whine started twenty seconds ago.
>
> And when the body cannot be saved, it was warned. The game does not stop; it restarts you at the autosave before the derelict, with nothing on the screen to mark it. You lost the interval. You did not lose what you learned: that a door in the wreck can blow, that the cold room needed its heater, that the haul had a sound.

**The emotion named: competent fragility.** Not a power fantasy. The pleasure is attunement — knowing, before any gauge says so, that the air has gone thin, that the haul on your back is the heavy one, that the lamp is about to die — and the dread is that the knowledge is all you have. The user's decision is the floor under it: death is lethal, including friendly fire — "This is like project zomboid, death is a bitch. It happens." — the body can fail, and when it does, it was warned.

**MDA mapping.** Challenge is delivered as *reading* competence, not reflex: the player masters a vocabulary of cues. Fantasy ("we are the only ones awake") is carried by the breath — in a dead derelict the only living sound is yours. Discovery is served thinly (which rooms are unbreathable is learned by the body). Sensation is the breath vapour in your own beam. *Self-determination:* Competence (the cue ladder is legible and learnable), Autonomy (go dark or keep the lamp; top up now or push one more room; carry the plate or leave it), Relatedness (Vertical Slice B: lighting a crewmate's visor is how you check on them, because the crew strip carries no vitals).

**Anchor moment (solo MVP, on a locked Graph fixture).** A derelict room on the DOOR-BREACH fixture (a door-sized opening on a V 20 room; LOW at about 5.6 s, DECOMP at about 14 s, VACUUM at about 36 s). *Illustrative placement: the fixture is the Graph's test case, not an authoring claim that any MVP derelict contains a door breach.* You are working a channel with the cutter in hand, lamp on.

> A latch somewhere lets go. The draught comes first — dust skating toward the hatch in your beam, a hiss at the frame (the Graph's witnesses for LOW, drawn by Failure & Trail). Within seconds your breath quickens and shortens: the air is thinning and your body says so. Then a click at your collar: the reserve valve. Your breathing goes close and dry — the room's air is no longer what you are breathing; your back is. No text appears. The numeral on the strip begins to fall in a way it never did while you were breathing the room.
>
> You have a minute and a half of yourself on your back, and you know it because you topped up at the cargo-bay tank before the pod hop and watched the sight-glass drop. You could finish the cut. You decide not to. You walk out, and halfway down the corridor the valve clicks again and the breath opens up: room air. The reserve stays where you left it. You will pay for this sortie at the tank, in ship O2 the scrubber will take minutes to give back.
>
> The other run of this moment: you stayed. The reserve runs down, the warning mark appears on the strip's breath dial (which has been showing the reserve since the valve clicked), your body stumbles (the stagger) and will not run. Then the reserve is gone, ten-odd seconds later you are on the deck, and the Controller's thirty-second window is all there is. Nobody hears it. The game restarts you at the autosave before the derelict. You lost twenty minutes. You did not lose the knowledge that a door in derelict B can blow.

**Timeline of the "stayed" branch** (illustrative; every duration is a `[PH]` value the Formulas section owns and the Graph harness confirms to ±15 %; timings derived from the DOOR-BREACH closed forms, `[derived; Graph harness to confirm]`; defaults: `RESERVE_S` 90 s, `RESERVE_WARN_S` 30 s `[PH]`, `RUNG_MIN_TICKS` 600 ticks = 10 s, `HYPOX_FAIL_S` 15 s `[PH]`; `p_o2` falls below the HYPOXIC boundary `HYPOXIA_PP` at about 6.4 s, *inside* the LOW band, not at DECOMP, because `p_o2` tracks total pressure):

| t (s) | Graph (true room) | Suit axis: breath | Body cue (consequence channel) | Reading (HUD; lie-capable architecture, honest in MVP) | Cap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | breach opens | OK | — (draught and hiss are the Graph's and Failure & Trail's) | O2 numeral starts to fall with the room | FULL |
| ~2.5 | `band_o2` THIN | **STRAINED** (the first cue; entry to STRAINED is not rate-limited) | breath quickens and shortens (thin signature) | numeral crosses a zone | FULL |
| ~5.6 | `band_p` LOW | STRAINED | draught and hiss at the opening (Graph/F&T) | — | FULL |
| ~6.4 | `band_o2` HYPOXIC (boundary `HYPOXIA_PP`) | STRAINED (source flips ROOM to RESERVE) | **valve click; breath switches to the closed reserve cadence; visor fog** | reserve reading becomes active | FULL |
| ~14 | `band_p` DECOMP (boundary `DECOMP_P`) | STRAINED | lean, draught audio | — | FULL |
| ~36 | VACUUM | STRAINED | closed cadence, fog | — | FULL |
| ~66 | — | target CRITICAL begins (reserve at or below `RESERVE_WARN_S` of air) | breath shortens again | — | FULL |
| ~76 | — | **CRITICAL** (condition held `RUNG_MIN_TICKS`) | **stagger** (falling edge, Controller's `STAGGER_TICKS` 30) then walk-only; fog dense | **warning mark on the strip's breath dial (showing the reserve)** | WALK_ONLY |
| ~96 | — | terminal condition begins (unbreathable, reserve 0) | breath laboured, no source | reserve reads empty | WALK_ONLY |
| ~111 | — | **FAILED** (terminal held `HYPOX_FAIL_S`) | collapse | — | NONE → DOWNED |
| ~141 | — | FAILED | — | — | DEAD at the Controller's `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 → Save/Load restart |

First body cue to FAILED is about 109 s here (the floor is `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S` 20 s). The worst case the limiter guarantees — a seat already at reserve 0 entering an unbreathable room — is first cue to FAILED of at least `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S`, then 30 s DOWNED. **Escape at any point before about 141 s works**: descent is never rate-limited (SE-17), so the run the seat stands in breathable air the breath axis drops to what the air demands and the cap rises; at `death_tick` the Controller checks the cap first.

**Second beat (derelict A, cold, the stowed plate).** The dead-bus wreck is unpowered and cold. Your breath smokes in your own cone (COOL); then rime creeps over your shoulder plates and you start to shiver (FROST); the panel's warmth reading is still in its first zone — your body is ahead of the number, which is the order the fantasy wants. You stow the 30 kg hull plate and your breathing deepens and slows, with a pack-creak under it (the load signature) — a different sound from thin air, which is shallower and faster. The haul has a sound. That sound is how, later (Vertical Slice A), you will know a "treasure" crate is garbage before you carry it home.

**Third beat (the falsifier; Vertical Slice A illustration only — MVP suit readings are not corrupted).** At low sanity the warmth reading glides up, slow and uncaused, with a violet fringe at its glyph (the art bible's HR-5 tell). Your breath is still smoking and you are shivering. You believe the shiver. You go home.

**Pillar alignment.** *Pillar 1 (primary), verbatim:* "Three channels are **inviolable** — no hallucination may ever alter them: **Physical consequences.** A pipe may *look* fixed, but the room still gets cold, the air still thins, the pressure still drops." The body is where "the room still gets cold" is felt: breath vapour, rime, shiver, visor fog and the stagger are that clause rendered on the player. *Design test:* "hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe → the pipe." Applied: the reserve is a stat, so it has physical witnesses (the valve click on engage, the breath changing source, the tank sight-glass dropping on top-up); a damaged suit is a stat, so below `SUIT_LEAK_HP` it *visibly leaks* (the seam vapour jet) — the pillar's own pipe, on a person. The human channel ("a crewmate's own light beam (colour and shape) are never faked") is why the beam shape is a per-seat, campaign-immutable identity and why Suit sets it exactly once. *Pillar 2, verbatim:* "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound, light, gauge drift, hull stress)." *Design test:* "breach fires at a hidden threshold vs. breach preceded by a 10–30 second escalating tell → the tell." The Graph's fairness gate covers the ship, not the body ("Player death is Suit's rule" (LS-13)), so Suit carries its own: `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S` from the first body cue to FAILED, enforced by the rung limiter; the low-cell tell `CELL_WARN_S` sits inside the same 10-30 s window. *Pillar 3, verbatim:* "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention ... Restraint and stealth are real strategies." The chest lamp is the largest exposure the player owns; a finite cell gives "go dark" a second, physical reason alongside Attention's. *Pillar 4, verbatim:* "**No lie is unfalsifiable**: the truth is always available through the physical world, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, the human channel, or time — and checking costs time, risk or trust." *Design test:* "a hallucination that can never be disproved vs. one that costs time, risk or trust to disprove → the costly one." *Secondary test, stated as a Suit rule:* "−10% move speed vs. a gauge showing a false value → the false gauge." This is why Suit imposes **no continuous penalty of any kind** — no speed scaling with O2, cold, load or integrity; only the three discrete cap states (FULL, WALK_ONLY, NONE), each with a body cue. A later stage that adds "slightly slower when cold" is reintroducing the rejected half of that test. *Pillar 5, verbatim:* "losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge." MVP death restarts from the newest verified autosave: the interval is lost, the lesson is not. *Design test:* "safe farming vs. scarcity that pushes inward → scarcity." The reserve and the cell are refilled only from real ship stock; nothing regenerates for free.

**Design tests (this system).**
1. **The body first (system test).** *When a vitals change could reach the player only through a reading vs. through a body cue they perceive without looking at the HUD → the body cue first.* Every rung change on every axis (breath, warmth, integrity) that has a physical consequence, and every weight-class step, has at least one closed-list body cue; the reading is its second opinion. **The one deliberate exclusion is integrity STRAINED** (`SUIT_LEAK_HP ≤ integrity_bp < SUIT_HP_OK`): that rung changes no cap, no insulation, no drain and no onset, so it has no body cue and needs none; it is carried by the integrity reading's zone band alone and nothing lethal hides there (SE-21, SE-22, OQ-19 ruled; CD-GDD-ALIGN 2026-10-26). Per-axis corollaries: engaging the reserve is audible on the run it happens (no silent engagement); every warmth rung above OK is visible on the sprite or audible before any damage ramp starts, and no readable body-temperature number exists; integrity damage without a visible landing is forbidden (below `SUIT_LEAK_HP` the suit visibly leaks, and that is also where the leak drain and the insulation loss begin, so no lethal effect of integrity precedes its witness); the three weight classes are three distinguishable breath steps; for every Graph fixture the elapsed time from the first body cue to FAILED is at least `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S`; and no Suit state scales speed, aim, channel rate or noise by a continuous amount.
2. **Cue or reading, never both unlabeled.** *When a new player-facing suit value is proposed vs. it is classed as exactly one of a closed-list body cue (never corruptible) or an HR-3-class reading (lie-capable by architecture) → classed as exactly one.* Rule SE-26 holds the list.
3. **The lamp costs something physical.** *When a free infinite flashlight vs. a beam the player rations → the rationed one* — within the Item Database's rule that scarcity comes from "O2, power, Attention and finite parts — not from tool upkeep": the cell's refill is ship power, not a part sink.

**Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks.** The split is the Graph's and the Emitter State's, applied to the body. The left column is guaranteed by construction (the block is `SIM`; cue state is a pure function of the occupant's compartment band enums and `suit.state`; no Suit code reads Sanity, Attention or any `PERCEPTION_OUT` view, CR-12). The right column is the five readings, which reach the HUD only through the Filter's `perceived`, which wraps `true_value(key, player_id)`, with keys `suit.o2`, `suit.reserve`, `suit.integrity`, `suit.warmth`, `suit.charge` — **architected lie-capable, not corrupted in the MVP.**

| Cannot lie (consequence channel — this GDD guarantees) | Can lie (reading register — Sanity and the Perception Filter only; Vertical Slice A at the earliest) |
|---|---|
| Breath: cadence, source (room versus reserve), load signature, vapour puff | The O2 dial arc and numeral (`suit.o2`) |
| The reserve valve click (breath family, see SE-26) | The reserve reading (`suit.reserve`) |
| Visor fog | The integrity reading (`suit.integrity`) |
| Rime on the shell plates; shiver | The warmth reading (`suit.warmth`) |
| Stagger, walk-only, DOWNED (the `locomotion_cap` effects) | The beam and tool charge reading and its cell-whine sound signature (`suit.charge`) |
| The seam vapour jet below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`; the HEAVY back-frame | Not here: the suppressant and glowstick rail counts are **anchors** with no `true_value` key, honest at MVP and Vertical Slice A (HUD HS-34, Sanity SN-19; OQ-6 closed) |
| The beam's colour, shape, and the moment it actually goes out (`force_off` at zero cell) | — |

**Required leak.** Any corrupted suit reading carries the art bible's HR-5 tri-modal tell: violet fringe gleam, one HR-5 motion signature (glide on a dial needle; continuous tick or stale on a numeral), a sound signature. Subtle is allowed; absent is not. Suit states this as a requirement on the HUD & Suit Layer, Sanity and the Perception Filter; it implements none of it.

**The falsifier ladder this hands the player (cheapest first).** (1) *Listen to your breath* — free, always available, works in the dark; thin air is fast and shallow, reserve is close and dry, load is slow and creaking. (2) *Look at your own body in your own light* — vapour shows in your cone; rime and fog are on your sprite; cost: the lamp is on (exposure, cell). (3) *Look at the room* — frost on surfaces, draught, the Graph's trails; cost: light and time. (4) *Move* — walk to a room you know is good; the valve clicks back, the shiver stops, the stagger lifts; cost: time and the sortie. (5) *A crewmate's visor in your beam* (Vertical Slice B, the human channel) — the crew strip relays no vitals; the check is lighting them; cost: trust and light. (6) *Time* — the reserve really empties, or it does not.

**Why the MVP keeps suit readings honest.** The MVP's one hallucination class (b) lives on ship instruments, where the room is the witness. A first-time player's only death clock should not lie before the cue ladder has been playtested — Pillar 2 protects learning before Pillar 4 tests it. The architecture is ready; the switch is Vertical Slice A's. *Risk carried:* if Vertical Slice A never turns it on, the "my numeral says warm, my breath says cold" moment — the best teaching beat this system offers Pillar 4 — never ships. Recorded as an Open Question for Sanity & Hallucination Rules.

**Guard against borrowing lies from the wrong source.** Hypoxia, cold and load produce *no* screen effect, tunnel, blur, double vision or "confusion" state. The body is honest even when it is failing; only Sanity lies. A hypoxia hallucination would be an unfalsifiable lie with the wrong owner.

**Tone and language.** *Use:* suit, the seal, breath, the reserve, the valve, top up, the tank, the cell, chest lamp, lamp, beam, go dark, visor, fog, rime, shiver, stagger, walk, down (downed), the hand (the tool in hand), the rail, stow, the haul, carry, the plate, the leak, patch. *Avoid:* meter words for the body (*health, HP, hit points, stamina* as a stat, *hunger, armour, encumbrance, weight* as a readout, *body temperature, oxygen meter, O2 bar*); inventory words (*inventory, backpack slots, bag, capacity, loadout screen*); light words already ruled by the Emitter State (*flashlight* — implies hand-held, the lamp is chest-mounted; *torch* for the beam — reserved for the Vertical Slice A deployable; *battery* for the suit's own store — that is a Graph node kind, the suit has a *cell*; *visibility, detection, stealth, light level*); and banned screen effects as vocabulary (*vignette, tunnel vision, blur, confusion, dizziness, screen frost*). *Register:* terse, physical, second person in fantasy prose; verbs of the body (breathe, shiver, stumble, carry); no heroic diction ("survive!") and no warning text on screen — the warning mark is a glyph, never words.

**Reference techniques — adopt and reject, precisely.**

| Reference | Adopt | Reject |
|---|---|---|
| **Pandorum** | The chest-mounted, hands-free lamp that swings with the body (user decision, binding); the suit as the claustrophobic, breathing frame of the experience. | A hand-held torch traded against tools (the hand point is lightless). |
| **Project Zomboid** | "Death is a bitch. It happens." (user): death is lethal, quiet and final for the interval; states are discrete, named by cause and escalate in steps. | The moodle icon strip as the primary channel — Suit's states live on the body (sprite, breath); the HUD carries readings only; no hunger, boredom or fatigue stats. |
| **Metro 2033 / Exodus** | The reading-versus-cue split: the filter timer is a reading on the wrist; the fogging visor and laboured breath are the body. Breath as the primary air cue. | Manual mask-wipe and filter-swap inputs (a chore verb; the reserve is topped up at the ship). |
| **Dead Space** | Diegetic state on the suit itself rather than floating UI. | The RIG health spine — a bar painted on the body is still a meter. Our on-body state is rime, fog and a leak jet, not a gauge. |
| **Subnautica** | The O2 numeral as a reading of a real reserve; breath audio as the warning. | Instant blackout at zero (we have the rung floor and the 30 s DOWNED window); tank-capacity upgrades (no progression). |
| **Barotrauma** | "Suit required" as a property of the place (the DECOMP band); pressure and oxygen as the room's clock. | The diving-suit speed penalty (the Pillar 4 secondary test); swapping loose tank items from inventory (top-up at a lit port instead). |
| **Death Stranding** | Load you can perceive on the body (the haul is felt). | The balance-and-stumble minigame and any mass-to-speed term (mass never changes speed or noise); cargo weight readout. |
| **Don't Starve** | Cold as a staged, legible threat with a lag before damage. | The screen-edge frost overlay (a vignette by another name) and the readable thermometer. |
| **Beyond the Aquila Rift** | The comforting false reality and the one physical detail that betrays it — the body as that detail. | Any authored "reveal" moment (anti-pillar: no authored story). |
| **Alien: Isolation / Amnesia** | Already ruled in the Emitter State's references; cited, not restated. | — |

**Anti-pillar guard rails (the Player Fantasy's close).**
1. **The suit has no weapon.** No tool carries damage, accuracy, range-as-damage or armour; the suppressant repels or distracts and its effect is Attention's and Horror's and **does not ship at MVP** (AT-19, HE-39; whether the item itself ships is OQ-34). *(Not a combat game.)*
2. **`apply_suit_damage` is a landing, never a decision.** Suit records harm; whether a crewmate may harm another is the Sanity gate applied in Interaction's `validate`. Suit never reads sanity. *(No intentional PvP.)*
3. **No progression on the body.** No insulation items, cell or reserve upgrades, shape unlocks or brightness levels. One suit, one kit, campaign-long. *(Not a freeform builder.)*
4. **No inventory and no weight readout.** The rail shows tools, suppressant charges, the glowstick count and the carried module; nothing else. Mass is heard in the breath, never read.
5. **No hidden stat without a witness.** Every suit stat that can kill (reserve, warmth load, integrity) has a body cue at each rung that has a physical consequence. Integrity STRAINED (`SUIT_LEAK_HP ≤ integrity_bp < SUIT_HP_OK`) is inert by design: cap FULL, no drain, full insulation (SE-21), so it carries no witness and hides nothing lethal; the first integrity consequence is the leak, and its witness is the seam jet on the same run. A rung with a consequence and no witness is forbidden (the P1 test: hidden stat versus visibly leaking pipe).
6. **No continuous penalty.** No speed, aim, channel or noise scaling from any vital; only the three cap states.
7. **No sanity or Attention indicator on any suit surface**, and no Suit cue driven by either.
8. **No screen effect from the body.** No vignette, tunnel, blur, frost overlay or hypoxia hallucination. The body shows on the sprite and in the sound.
9. **No mercy timer, no self-revive, no death screen.** Recovery while DOWNED happens only when an axis genuinely improves; death restarts from the autosave with no beat, no lights-out, no death screen. The fairness is in the tell floor, not in a second chance.
10. **No free air or light.** The reserve and the cell refill only from real ship stock at a lit port (the dormant PASSIVE configuration, OQ-2, refills the reserve from the room's real O2 and still costs it); no passive regeneration of integrity either (a passive mend would erase attrition and is a hidden regeneration).

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Conventions.* Rules are `SE-n`; formulas `D.n`; acceptance criteria `AC-SE-n`; restore reasons `SUIT_RESTORE_*`; diagnostics `suit_*`. `[PH]` marks a placeholder that is registered with a range (Tuning Knobs) and is never a fact until the pacing spike confirms it. "(provisional — X undesigned)" marks a contract with a system whose GDD does not yet exist (Interaction, Ship Section & Layout and 2D Lighting & Occlusion were formerly in flight and are now designed; their rows quote current text); "must repeat" names what that GDD has to restate (the Graph's own rule for provisional rows). "(orchestrator ruling: ...)" marks a place where two specialist proposals disagreed and the stage rulings, or failing that a stated choice, decided. The four Graph band constants appear **only by name** (`COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC`, `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC`, `HYPOXIA_PP`, `DECOMP_P`); Suit reads them from the registry and never restates a value (the Graph's source-lint, AC-SE-1). Every rule carries *Rule*, *Why*, *Rejected* and *Criterion*, and is tied to a pillar. Two structural tests run through all of them: **every mutator Suit publishes changes only `suit.state`**, and **every command Suit registers is `equip_tool`**.

*Dependency status used throughout (re-checked at the round-1 revision, 2026-10-26, Glob of `design/gdd/`).* Designed: Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Item & Module Database, Player Controller & Input, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Save/Load & Versioned Format (also Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera and Failure & Trail, cited only). **Designed 2026-10-26 (18:38-19:34); contract current:** `interaction-system.md` (19:34; re-verified at the final advisor review, 2026-10-27), `ship-section-and-layout-data-model.md` (18:38), `2d-lighting-and-occlusion.md` (18:48). **Designed since (re-checked at the consistency pass, 2026-10-27):** Attention, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Boarding & Salvage, HUD & Suit Layer, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Horror Entities, Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells, Web Map & Transit, Chart Table, Modular Ship Assembly and Debug Overlay (True Values); their rows quote current text where a routed ask reached this GDD. **No file:** Co-op Session & Replication (Vertical Slice B) and Failure Contract & Re-wake (Vertical Slice). Every statement below about an absent system is a requirement this GDD places on it; statements about the three designed neighbours quote their current text.

#### Group A — Block, hook, keying, room

**SE-1. One block, one hook.** *Rule.* Suit registers exactly one state block, **`suit.state`** (`class SIM`, `persist true`, `persist_scope WORLD`, `scope PER_PLAYER` keyed by PlayerId, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY` per SE-36, `schema_version 1`, `supported_versions [1]`, `added_in_format 1`, `restore_after []`), and exactly one step hook, **`suit.vitals`** — divisor 6, **offset 0**, `after: [graph.propagate.atmo]` and nothing else. It never declares `after: emitter.place`. Registration is frozen at boot (CR-6): an unknown `after` id or a cycle fails boot, and there is no alias concept. The hook chain the Controller depends on is `suit.vitals` → `controller.step` → `body.integrate` → `emitter.place`; `controller.step` declares `after: suit.vitals`, `emitter.place` declares `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]`, so Suit's own declaration is the single edge to the Graph. `restore_after` is empty because `post_restore` runs after all blocks restore (CR-8), so the `carry_ref` invariant check (SE-13) needs no restore ordering. If Vertical Slice A staggers the Graph's hooks (the Graph names a two-registration change), Suit's registration moves with `graph.propagate.atmo` — that is a boot-time data change, not a runtime one. *(The kernel's example "Suit (6, 4)" is a boot-warning fixture for never-co-running hooks, not Suit's registration; the kernel itself calls the shared (6, 0) "normal" and the Graph fixes its three hooks at (6, 0). The provisional block name `vitals.state` in the Controller and Save/Load drafts is replaced by `suit.state`; the sweep is a consistency-pass item, Cross-References.)* *Why.* Every designed block is `<owner>.state` and the kernel's velocity rule wants one coarse block per domain system; offset 0 gives Suit the Graph's same-tick atmosphere, whereas offset 4 would read the previous run's. *Rejected.* Two blocks (`vitals` and `loadout`) — violates the velocity rule; offset 4; `after: emitter.place` — creates a cycle through the Controller. *Criterion.* AC-SE-2 (hook-order list) and AC-SE-3 (boot with an unknown `after` fails). *Pillar.* P1 — the body reads the true same-tick room.

**SE-2. Seats are keyed by PlayerId.** *Rule.* Every per-seat field lives inside dictionaries keyed by PlayerId (an int). No `peer_id`, node path or engine object appears in the block; the persistent keys are PlayerId and the item `def_id` only. *Why.* Peer ids are reassigned per connection and the kernel corrects the index's "keyed by peer ID" wording; seat identity must survive a rejoin and a restore. *Rejected.* Peer-keyed records. *Criterion.* AC-SE-4 (schema lint: no key shaped like a peer id; snapshot round-trip with a re-bound peer). *Pillar.* Serves the co-op seam (VS-B) without building it.

**SE-3. A seat's compartment.** *Rule.* Each run, for each bound seat that is not DEAD, Suit resolves the occupant's compartment: it reads the seat's `(section_id, pos_px)` from Ship Body's read view (`section_id` from `actor_section(actor_id)`, BDY-29, derived on demand), as of T-1 (`suit.vitals` runs before `body.integrate`, so the position is up to one tick stale plus the hook's own up-to-five-tick cadence), calls Layout's pure read **`room_at(section_id, px) -> room_id | NONE`**, then the Graph's `compartment_at(room_ref)` and `compartment(cid)` for the band enums `band_o2`, `band_p`, `band_t` and the raw temperature `T`. Suit keys every cue off the **enums** (hysteresis is inherited from the Graph's `atmosphere_derive`); the raw `T` is read only for the warmth integrator's shifted-onset comparison (SE-20), against the registry names. *(orchestrator ruling: Layout's signature is adopted. The stage ruling R28 proposed a body-id form `room_at(body_id, pos_px)`; Layout's designed text publishes `room_at(section_id, px)`, so Suit takes Layout's form and Ship Body must supply `section_id`.)* **The last known room** (persisted as `last_section_id` and `last_room_ref`) **is kept for the pod only.** Layout's designed text says (LY-5): "the **sill** … is not a tile and does not count in `V`: each door declares a `sill_room` … and `room_at(section_id, px)` answers `sill_room` for a px on the sill, so there is no walkable point in a section where `room_at` answers `NONE`." A NONE therefore never occurs at a walkable point inside a section; on a Layout body it is a defect-only state (Ship Body BDY-29: CI asserts it never occurs in a clean build). A seat with no room at spawn is an authoring lint failure, not a runtime state. **Two cases, keyed on the Controller's `loco_state`:** (i) **`loco_state` IN_POD**, whatever `room_at` answers (NONE, or a room of the pod's section) — **HOLD_ROOM**: the pod's Graph section is dormant and never breathed, so Suit calls **no `draw_air`** (M15 on a dormant section returns 0 and would raise `breath_starved`), the reserve and the cell are held constant, and the thermal load and the hold counters do not advance (SE-35; Web Map & Transit WM-17, ASK-S1); (ii) **`room_at` answers NONE with `loco_state` not IN_POD** — **EXPOSED**: the seat is exposed to the EXTERIOR (vacuum, cold), **never to the last room's bands**; by rule the air class is unbreathable with `band_p` VACUUM and `band_o2` ANOXIC (source RESERVE while the reserve lasts, then NONE; no `draw_air`), and the warmth integrator reads the Graph's EXTERIOR ambient `T_AMB_MC` (LS-3) as its raw `T`, with the `band_t` that `atmosphere_derive` gives it; everything keeps running. *(Ship Body's must-repeat, BDY-16 and BDY-29, CD-GDD-ALIGN: a body standing outside the hull must not breathe the hull's air (P1). Web Map's ASK-S1 wording ("`room_at` answers NONE **or** `loco_state == IN_POD`") is applied together with Body's ruling: IN_POD enters HOLD_ROOM; a bare NONE is EXPOSED, not a hold. The EXTERIOR values are taken by rule from LS-3; whether `compartment(cid)` is readable on the EXTERIOR node is a routed Graph confirmation, OQ-12 (e).)* *Why.* Room polygons are Layout's and atmosphere is the Graph's; Suit owns neither. *Rejected.* Suit keeping its own room polygons; sampling a light level to decide "room". *Criterion.* AC-SE-5 (a seat on a sill takes the bands of `sill_room`; a seat that crosses a door changes bands within one run; a NONE with `loco_state` not IN_POD is EXPOSED to the EXTERIOR; an IN_POD seat holds). *Pillar.* P1. *(Layout's designed text publishes `room_at(section_id, px)` as used here; Ship Body supplies `section_id` through `actor_section` (BDY-29); the pod is a dormant Graph section, never breathed (WM-17).)*

#### Group B — Breath, reserve, top-up

**SE-4. Breath source is a pure function of band enums, the reserve and the room.** *Rule.* Each run each seat picks its **breath source** from the enums of the occupant's compartment and its reserve — never from a grant that came back:

| Air class (from the enums) | Reserve | Source | What Suit does | Breath-axis target (SE-19) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **AIR_OK**: `band_o2` not in {HYPOXIC, ANOXIC} **and** `band_p` not in {DECOMP, VACUUM} | any | **ROOM** | calls Graph `draw_air(cid, R_BREATH)` (M15, Class B) for the seat | by the band (OK, or STRAINED for THIN / LOW) |
| **Unbreathable** (the complement) | above 0 | **RESERVE** | debits `min(R_BREATH, reserve)` from the reserve; calls nothing | STRAINED, or CRITICAL at or below `RESERVE_WARN_MGU` |
| Unbreathable, `band_o2` HYPOXIC and `band_p` in {NORMAL, LOW} | 0 | **GASP** | calls `draw_air(cid, R_BREATH)` — whatever the room still holds | CRITICAL (never FAILED) |
| Unbreathable, any other combination (ANOXIC, DECOMP, VACUUM) | 0 | **NONE** | nothing is breathed | terminal condition (FAILED after the hold) |

Because the HYPOXIC and DECOMP boundaries *are* `HYPOXIA_PP` and `DECOMP_P`, this rule uses the four named constants only through the enums, and no numeric literal of a band value appears in Suit (the Graph's source-lint passes by construction). The source is a function of enums and the reserve only: the 0.1 s lag of the Graph's queued grant (SE-5) can never open a window in which a seat is on room air, granted nothing, and not yet on the reserve. *(checkpoint ruling CK-1, 2026-10-26: GASP accepted as the single departure from R4/R14 — HYPOXIC is the Graph's "stamina loss" band and ANOXIC its "rapid incapacitation" band; the source stays a pure function of the band enums and the reserve, and FAILED requires ANOXIC, DECOMP or VACUUM with an empty reserve. Logged; OQ-1.)* *Why.* P1: the room's clock is the body's clock. The Graph's survival clocks (O2-ROOM, O2-SHIP, DOOR-BREACH) stay the player's clocks; the reserve extends the retreat window by `RESERVE_S` and does not replace them. *Rejected.* (a) A sealed tank from the first breath (makes the visible O2 graph irrelevant to a suited crew); (c) a fixed non-refillable reserve (every sortie is permanent attrition on a number the player cannot restore, and it contradicts the concept's prep step "Prep (O₂, tools, suppressants)"); choosing the source from the grant (flaps on a one-run-late value). *Criterion.* AC-SE-6 (fixture sweep over the Graph's DOOR-BREACH, O2-ROOM, O2-SHIP: the reserve engages at the first run the enums say unbreathable and never earlier) and AC-SE-1 (literal lint). *Pillar.* P1, P5 (scarcity pushes inward).

**SE-5. Respiration ledger and the one-run lag.** *Rule.* The Graph queues `draw_air` to phase 0 of the next Graph run, applied in ascending `(compartment id, crew id)` against a running balance, and returns the shortfall `request − granted` to Suit, which owns "what a lungful of nothing does". Suit stores `pending_draw_mgu` (the request just issued for ROOM or GASP, else 0). On the following run it reconciles against the Graph's published `draw_grant(crew)` (`{requested, granted}`): the grant is attributed to the breath first, `breath_granted = min(granted, pending_draw_mgu)`, and `shortfall = pending_draw_mgu − breath_granted` (the breath part only: a room that grants exactly `R_BREATH` owes the reserve nothing, whatever extra was asked, D.2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; it debits `min(shortfall, reserve)` from the reserve (diagnostic `suit_reserve_shortfall`); if the reserve cannot cover the shortfall it sets the one-run flag `breath_starved`, which raises the breath-axis demand to CRITICAL for that run (D.3). Seats submit in ascending PlayerId. The 0.1 s lag is accepted and stated. *Graph confirmation (answered, Life-Support Graph LS-18, M15):* `draw_air` is queued to the next Graph run's phase 0 and the grant is published as `draw_grant(crew)` → `{requested, granted}` until the following run, so the one-run lag of this rule is the real behaviour and Suit reads `draw_grant(crew).granted` on its next run. The rule would also hold if the return were synchronous, because SE-4 never uses the grant to choose the source. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Why.* Closes the seam without a Graph change. *Rejected.* Switching the source on the grant. *Criterion.* AC-SE-7: request 500,000 mGU against `n_o2` 300,000: granted 300,000, shortfall 200,000 debited from the reserve one run late; two seats in one room receive grants in ascending PlayerId order. *Pillar.* P1.

**SE-6. The reserve.** *Rule.* Each seat carries `reserve_mgu` in `0..RESERVE_MAX_MGU`, with `RESERVE_MAX_MGU = RESERVE_S × (TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS) × R_BREATH` — 270,000 mGU, 90 s of breathing, at the defaults (`RESERVE_S` 90 `[PH]`, range 45-240). `RESERVE_WARN_MGU` is computed the same way from `RESERVE_WARN_S` `[PH]`; both are derived on read from the time knobs and `R_BREATH` and never stored, so changing `R_BREATH` keeps the felt duration (the Item DB's F-14 ladder reads `R_BREATH`). Its debits are exactly three: the engaged-reserve spend (`R_BREATH` per run while the source is RESERVE), the leak (SE-22) and the one-run-late shortfall of a room that granted less than the breath request (SE-5, diagnostic `suit_reserve_shortfall`); its credits are exactly the V7 top-up (SE-7) and, only in the dormant PASSIVE configuration, the granted part of the room-air extra (D.2). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Its **physical witnesses** are mandatory (P1: a stat with no witness is the hidden stat): a valve click on engage and on disengage, a breath-cadence step (SE-26), and the sight-glass drop on the Graph's O2_TANK at top-up (drawn by In-World Instruments, Lighting and the Graph's stock). `R_BREATH` **300 mGU per step per crew, range 100-600** is a Suit-owned constant: it is today a Graph placeholder ("owned by Suit & Equipment") and is registered here. *Exported to Derelict Authoring:* every authored vacuum traverse must be walkable (or carried, at the slower carry speed) in no more than `RESERVE_S / 2`, and `RESERVE_WARN_S ≤ RESERVE_S / 2`, so an authored traverse never reaches CRITICAL. *Why.* A buffer denominated in time survives a rate change; an authoring bound keeps vacuum sections a bounded, designable thing. *Rejected.* Storing seconds (loses the Graph's own unit, which the top-up draws in). *Criterion.* AC-SE-8a to 8c (the reserve never exceeds its maximum or goes negative; every fall is exactly the sum of the three debits; the felt duration survives an `R_BREATH` change). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Pillar.* P1, P5.

**SE-7. Top-up is Interaction's verb V7; Suit supplies the credit side.** *Rule.* Refilling the reserve (at a lit O2_TANK port) and the cell (at a lit BATTERY port) is a world-targeted verb, so it is **Interaction's**: its lit, reach and reason machinery are Interaction's, and Suit does not register a world-targeted command as the primary path. This GDD writes the V7 row in full so Interaction can adopt it verbatim:

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Command type | `top_up` (owner Interaction; origin PLAYER; `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`; `coalesce NONE`; `max_age_ticks` 120; **not** `allowed_when_paused`) |
| Payload | `{node_id}` (an O2_TANK or BATTERY port subject) |
| Mode | TAP. Interaction declines the fixed-duration CHANNEL option (IA-9: no new constant and no bar); `TOPUP_CHANNEL_TICKS` is retained as data only |
| Tool | none (bare hand) |
| Validate chain (all immediate class) | kernel authority → `in_reach` → `lit` → `BUSY` (carrying, channelling, staggered, downed) → node kind in {O2_TANK, BATTERY} → **`ALREADY_FULL`** (Suit-namespaced: `reserve_deficit_mgu(player_id)` is 0 for an O2_TANK port, or `cell_deficit_ticks(player_id)` is 0 for a BATTERY port — the actor's own state, so immediate) |
| Apply (O2_TANK) | `granted = withdraw_store(node_id, reserve_deficit_mgu)` (Graph M16, Class B — Suit is a declared caller), then Suit's `credit_reserve(player_id, granted)` |
| Apply (BATTERY) | the deficit in ticks converted to stock units by `CELL_STOCK_PER_KTICK` `[PH]` (rounded up), `granted = withdraw_store(node_id, units)`, then `credit_cell(player_id, granted converted back, rounded down)` |
| Outcome if `granted` is 0 | applied-and-did-nothing — no token, no distinct cue, `credit_*` is not called; the sight-glass not falling and the reserve reading not rising are the answer (Interaction ruling, IA-12: a TAP has no deferred class); the gauge is module-body and may be wrong, the world answers |
| Noise / Attention | none from Suit; an M16 stock debit is not a power draw and never appears in `power_use` (Graph LS-18, confirmed 2026-10-27), so the top-up is invisible to Attention by construction |

A top-up never credits more than the deficit; for an O2_TANK port the stock falls by exactly the credit, and for a BATTERY port by `ceil(credited_ticks × CELL_STOCK_PER_KTICK / 1000)` plus the partial-grant dust of D.9 (zero on a complete grant). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The V7 switch — USER DECISION (OQ-2)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Two GDDs disagree until the user rules: this GDD's `TOPUP_MODE` default is VERB, while the Life-Support Graph's Open Question 20 (owner: user) says that until decided Suit runs `TOPUP_MODE` PASSIVE with `CELL_ENABLED` 0, and V7 is absent from the Graph's LS-26 MVP verb list. The choice decides whether the lamp cell and the top-up ritual exist at MVP, so it is a scope decision, not a tuning detail; the creative-director recommends VERB. Until it is ruled both configurations stay specified and correct (D.2 and D.9 carry the PASSIVE credit term). Interaction adopts V7 `top_up` verbatim as a TAP (its CHANNEL option declined) and marks it MVP pending the Graph's LS-26 amendment (interaction-system.md IA-9); `TOPUP_MODE` PASSIVE remains the fallback only if the Graph does not amend LS-26 (OQ-2, owner Graph and producer). This GDD ships two data-selectable configurations through one knob, `TOPUP_MODE` `[PH]`: **VERB** (default; requires V7 in the Graph's MVP verb list) and **PASSIVE** (the fallback, selected only if the Graph does not add V7). In PASSIVE mode (i) the reserve refills passively from breathable room air — while `band_o2` is NORMAL and the room's `p_o2` stays at or above `TOPUP_MIN_PP` `[PH]` after the draw, Suit adds a `draw_air` of `TOPUP_PASSIVE_MGU_PER_RUN` `[PH]` to its ROOM request and, one run later, credits the part of the Graph's grant above `R_BREATH` (D.2: the breath is served first, so the extra is credited only from what exceeds the breath request, and a room that grants exactly `R_BREATH` costs the reserve nothing) (the guard exists because a 270,000 mGU fill would drop a V = 8 room from its normal partial pressure to about 176 dkPa, below the THIN boundary); and (ii) the cell is disabled (`CELL_ENABLED` 0, SE-8). Either configuration is data-only in the block; the passive path is not recommended and exists so that the Graph's MVP verb list (LS-26) cannot block the MVP. *(Interaction's designed text adopts this row verbatim, IA-9; the one open point is the Graph's LS-26 amendment, OQ-2.)* *Why.* Prep becomes a visible ritual that spends real stock; the reserve and the cell are two symmetric prep gauges. *Rejected.* A Suit-registered `top_up` command (absorbs lit/reach/reason machinery); a passive-only reserve as the default (prep is trivial and hides the ritual). *Criterion.* AC-SE-9a to 9e. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Pillar.* P1, P5.

#### Group C — Beam, shape, glowsticks

**SE-8. The beam cell.** *Rule.* Each seat carries `cell_ticks` in `0..CELL_MAX_TICKS`, `CELL_MAX_TICKS = CELL_S × TICK_RATE` — 72,000 ticks, **1200 s** of lit beam, at the defaults (`CELL_S` 1200 `[PH]`, range 600-2400). Each run drains `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (6) ticks while the seat's beam `emitter.state.lit` is true **as of T-1**; at zero Suit calls `force_off(beam_id)` (E5, Class B), which lands before the same tick's `emitter.place`. `toggle_beam.validate` reads Suit's `charge(player_id)` and rejects a turn-ON at zero with `CHARGE_EMPTY` (immediate class); a turn-OFF is always accepted. **Output is constant until empty — there is no dim**: the Emitter State's EM-8 ("no brightness dial") and its Open Question 16 are answered "no"; Suit never sets `output_pm`. Low-charge tell: the cell-whine audio cue and the warning mark beside the charge glyph start at `CELL_WARN_S` **20 s** `[PH]` (range 10-30, inside Pillar 2's window) while the beam is untouched until `force_off`. The up-to-five-tick over- or under-drain per toggle at divisor 6 is accepted and stated. The cell powers nothing else in the MVP. **Fallback (data-only):** `CELL_ENABLED` 0 — a distinct knob, never a sentinel value of `CELL_S` (0 means an empty cell everywhere else in this GDD): the cell never drains, and `CHARGE_EMPTY` stays registered and never fires. *Adopted text (the Emitter State's EM-10 and its Interactions row, "whichever GDD lands second adopts the other's text"):* "Suit & Equipment owns charge, drain, `CHARGE_EMPTY` and the HUD 'beam and tool charge' reading. `toggle_beam.validate` reads Suit's charge view and rejects a turn-ON at zero charge with `CHARGE_EMPTY`; a turn-OFF is always accepted. Suit's divisor-6 `suit.vitals` reads `emitter.state.lit` on the seat's beam to drain charge and calls `force_off(beam_id)` (E5) at zero." The Emitter State must repeat: Suit "keeps the hook name `suit.vitals` and its `after: graph.propagate.atmo`". *Routed asks (not Suit rules).* (i) Answered 2026-10-27: the Controller adapter holds `BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS` (registry; 10 ticks, range 10-20, PC-5) between two submitted `toggle_beam`, which exceeds the six-tick (`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`) window: any lit span of six consecutive ticks contains exactly one sample point of the divisor-6 read, so with the debounce a toggling player cannot light the beam for free between samples; without it a span of four or fewer ticks can avoid every sample (the simulation adds no strobe cooldown). (ii) The Graph owner confirms that M16 `withdraw_store` is legal on a BATTERY node's `stock`. *Why.* P3 needs the lamp to cost something physical; the Item DB's IDB-29 ("scarcity … not from tool upkeep") is honoured because the refill is ship power, not a part sink. *Rejected.* Dimming (changes `output_pm`, exposure and `lit`; the beam is the human-channel anchor); passive recharge under fixtures (free energy that couples Suit to Emitter per-kind data). *Criterion.* AC-SE-10: a continuous 72,000-tick lit run reaches 0 and `force_off` fires exactly once; a turn-ON at 0 is rejected `CHARGE_EMPTY`. *Pillar.* P3, P2.

**SE-9. Beam shape is a loadout fact fixed at campaign start.** *Rule.* `suit.state.beam_shape` is in 1..4 (`BEAM_WIDE` 1, `BEAM_NARROW` 2, `BEAM_SPLIT` 3, `BEAM_FAN` 4, by the Emitter State's `shape_id`). At new game, the host session owner calls **`bind_beam(player_id, beam_shape)`** (E4, Class A) in the same CR-7 boot step that enters the seat beam from `create_seat_beam` (E9) in the binding table. There is no change-shape action (the Controller and EM-8 say so) and the shape is immutable for the campaign. Seat 1 defaults to `BEAM_WIDE` (the most legible cone: 30°, 6 tiles); seat n defaults to `shape_id` n so beam identity is unique among crew. An optional new-campaign picker is the Chart Table's (CT-24 reserves a `NewCampaignOptions` seam for Vertical Slice B; no picker at MVP); the choice persists in `suit.state`. *Why.* The beam's colour and shape are the human-channel anchor; a per-seat identity that cannot change is how a crewmate's light is recognised. *Rejected.* An in-run shape swap (a freeform-builder drift and a Pillar 3 loophole); a per-shape charge cost (no progression). *Criterion.* AC-SE-11: `bind_beam` is called once per seat at new game and never at runtime. *Pillar.* P1 (human channel), P3.

**SE-10. Glowsticks are a counted stack.** *Rule.* Each seat holds at most one `cons_glowstick` stack, capped by the registry's `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` (4), so the per-seat carry cap is 4. The start count is **`GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT` 2** `[PH]` (range 0-4; Suit-owned — "the start count is not an Item DB field"). A stowed stick is inert and does not age (the Item DB). Interaction's `throw_glowstick` validates `stowed_count(player_id, cons_glowstick)` (its reason `OUT_OF_CHARGES`) and its apply calls `spend_stowed(player_id, cons_glowstick, 1)`; the landing light exists only on landing, created by the Emitter State's deployable mutator. A pickup into a full stack is refused by Suit's `add_stowed` (reason `STACK_FULL`) and the item stays in the world. **I-SUPPLY (the Emitter State's supply invariant):** seats × stack cap must be ≤ `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` (registry 8, range 4-16): one seat × 4 = 4 passes; four seats × 4 = 16 fails — **CONFLICT recorded for Vertical Slice B** (raise the cap within its range, or accept the visible evict-wane); D.8 A11 is the build assertion (a boot failure at one seat, a boot warning above one seat; Interaction's Open Question 15 records the same I-SUPPLY, EM:938), and multi-seat test fixtures state `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` 16. `THROW_RANGE_TILES` and `THROW_ARC_TICKS` belong to Interaction. *Why.* Rationed light is the Pillar 3/5 lever; one stack keeps supply small and the cap clean. *Rejected.* Two stacks (cap 8) — over-supplies the scarcest lever; a per-seat cap of 2 for four seats only. *Criterion.* AC-SE-12: pickup #5 into a stack of 4 is refused and the item remains in the world; I-SUPPLY evaluates 4 ≤ 8 at one seat. *Pillar.* P3, P5.

#### Group D — Tools, holders, carry

**SE-11. Tool roster and `equip_tool`.** *Rule.* The hand rail's tool slots are fixed: **1 `tool_cutter`, 2 `tool_welder`, 3 `tool_scanner`, 4 `tool_suppressant`**; slots 5 and 6 are unbound in the MVP. Suit registers exactly one command type, **`equip_tool {slot: 1..6}`** — origin PLAYER, `actor_binding OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `coalesce NONE`, `max_age_ticks` 120 (the Controller's discrete-verb value), payload within `MAX_PAYLOAD`; the Controller's `tool_cycle` input is resolved by the Adapter to a slot (the Adapter, not the simulation, picks "next"). `validate` (immediate class, in this order): kernel authority; **`NOT_ACTIONABLE`** in STAGGERED, DOWNED or DEAD; **`BUSY`** while carrying a module or channelling; **`EMPTY_SLOT`** for slot 5 or 6 or a tool the seat does not hold. `apply` sets `equipped_slot`; equipping the already-equipped slot is an accepted no-op. **Sim swap duration 0 ticks:** `apply` sets `equipped_slot` in the tick the command is applied, and `tool_in_hand` answers the new tool from that tick; the Controller's hand animation is presentation only and gates no command (so Sanity's rung-2 scan cost `T_EQUIP`, D.7, is exactly 0 ticks, OQ-SN-6); no noise; **never `allowed_when_paused`** (SE-32). The hand rail also shows the glowstick count and the carried module as passive entries (SE-30). *(Interaction's designed command table lists `equip_tool` as Suit's only command and registers no `use_item` (AC-IA-1); `use_item` is routed to the kernel and Item DB owners as a stale token. A use verb that acts on the world is Interaction's and calls Suit's `consume_charge`; the suppressant's effect is Attention's and Horror's.)* *Why.* A tool slot is a Suit fact; what a tool does is the system it acts on. *Rejected.* A holster slot 0 (no MVP verb needs a holstered state; bare-hand verbs declare no tool requirement — Interaction's); equip time (a tell-window exploit in either direction); auto-equip on target (removes a Suit decision and makes `equip_tool` pointless). *Criterion.* AC-SE-13: `equip_tool` on a carrying seat answers `BUSY`; slot 5 answers `EMPTY_SLOT`; while paused it is rejected. *Pillar.* P2 (no equipping with time stopped mid-tell), anti-pillar not a combat game (a slot, not a loadout screen).

**SE-12. Holder model.** *Rule.* Suit owns the three holder classes defined by the Item DB (IDB-16 "the Item DB defines what a thing is; the holder owns who has it and how many"): **HAND** (tool instances — unique per player, one of each `tool_*`, `stack_max` 1), **STOWED** (counted entries in the block, one stack per `def_id`, merged per IDB-15 when `def_id`, `condition_bp` and `charge` are equal), and **CARRIED** (the single carried instance — the Controller owns `carry_ref`, Suit owns the instance and the stored `carried_id`). All modules and the curio are CARRIED; tools are HAND; parts and glowsticks are STOWED. A refused merge leaves the surplus where it was ("the merge is refused by the holder (Suit) and the surplus stays where it was"). The persisted instance is `{def_id, count, condition_bp, cap_bp, patched_at_step, charge}` with canonical zeros; masses and classes are looked up from the static catalogue and never saved. **Suit mints no ids**: the only id it stores is the carried one, passed in by `set_carried`; stowed stacks carry no ids. There is no pack capacity and no weight limit — the carrying cost is the mass cue (SE-28) and, for a carried module, the Controller's `V_CARRY` (no run). *Why.* One owner for "who holds what"; a pack grid or weight cap is an inventory screen by another name (Item DB OQ 6). *Rejected.* A pack grid or total-weight cap. *Criterion.* AC-SE-14: a merge-and-refuse table test over `part_hull_plate` (×1), `part_duct_coupling` (×4) and `cons_glowstick` (×4). *Pillar.* P5, anti-pillar no inventory.

**SE-13. The carried instance and the one mover.** *Rule.* `set_carried(player_id, instance_id, instance)` stores the instance; `release_carried(player_id)` removes it and returns `(instance_id, instance)`. **Suit never self-releases.** On `locomotion_cap` NONE the Controller clears `carry_ref` and **Interaction** converts it to a loose item next tick by calling `release_carried`. Stagger does not drop the module; downed does. `post_restore` checks `controller.state.carry_ref == suit.state.carried_id` (or both NONE) and fails `SUIT_RESTORE_CARRY_MISMATCH` otherwise. The loose-world owner and the instance-id counter are not Suit's: Interaction's designed IA-28 owns `loose[]` and the id counter in `interaction.state`; Suit stores only the id it is handed, and its text holds under any owner. *Why.* Exactly one mover prevents a module existing in two holders at once. *Rejected.* Suit allocating ids (two allocators collide). *Criterion.* AC-SE-15: a carry dropped on DOWNED round-trips to a loose item with identical instance fields; a restore with a mismatched `carry_ref` fails with the named reason. *Pillar.* P1 (the world holds each thing once).

**SE-14. The suppressant.** *Rule.* `SUPPRESSANT_START_CHARGES` **3** `[PH]` = the Item DB's `charges_max`. The suppressant's effect and verb are Attention's and Horror's; Suit owns only the charge count, decremented by `consume_charge(player_id, tool_suppressant)`. An empty suppressant stays on the hand rail, greyed — a reading, not a banner. A pickup of a fresh `tool_suppressant` while the held one is at 0 **swaps** through `replace_tool(player_id, instance) -> discarded` (the empty one is discarded, not dropped; a loose duplicate of a held tool is an authoring warning); a swap while the held one has charges left is refused. There is no refill source in the MVP. **At MVP the suppressant has no effect, full or empty** (Attention AT-19): Attention defines only the contract — the ordered read `tool_charge`, the registrant's `consume_charge`, and `OUT_OF_CHARGES` — and the effect is a Vertical Slice addendum; Horror ships none (HE-39). Keeping `tool_suppressant` in the MVP start kit is a **USER DECISION (OQ-34)**: Attention calls it a producer scope call (OQ-AT-5), Horror's C-HE-6 / AC-HE-124 asks for its removal (its own text now records that nothing has been ruled, Options A and B), and the creative-director recommends removal (Option A) because a visible, equippable tool with no registered verb is a false affordance against Pillar 1 and the anti-pillar "the suit has no weapon". Until the user rules, this GDD applies neither option and slot 4 stands as written; OQ-34 carries the exact edit list for each option. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Why.* A unique-per-player tool would otherwise make a second suppressant an unpickable trap. *Rejected.* A refill station (no vendor or refill faucet exists); dropping the empty one (a loose duplicate). *Criterion.* AC-SE-16. *Pillar.* P5.

**SE-15. The starting kit.** *Rule.* Granted once in `default_state()` at `new_game` from `assets/data/suit/start_kit.json` (integers only; one file per class, the Item DB's IDB-26 convention): the four tools (suppressant 3/3), `GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT` glowsticks, a full reserve and a full cell, `beam_shape` per SE-9, and `equipped_slot` (default 2, the welder — the verb of the first ten minutes). This is the "one-time starting kit" faucet the Item DB names; salvage is every other faucet. *Why.* The first ten minutes ("see the leak, follow it to the source, fix it") need the welder and the cutter in hand; unique tools held from the start avoid a cannot-pick-up trap. *Rejected.* A minimal kit with the scanner and suppressant found on derelict A (moves a tutorial dependency into authored placement). *Criterion.* AC-SE-17: the new-game block equals the data file; a restore of the same save is byte-equal. *Pillar.* P5.

#### Group E — The vitals ladder, tells and death

**SE-16. Three axes, four rungs, one cap.** *Rule.* Suit tracks three independent axes — **breath, warmth, integrity** — each at rung OK 0, STRAINED 1, CRITICAL 2 or FAILED 3. `vitals_state` is the worst axis. `locomotion_cap` is FULL for OK and STRAINED, WALK_ONLY for CRITICAL, NONE for FAILED, **derived on read and never saved**. The Controller reads only `locomotion_cap` (its lint). *Why.* Three physical sources, three witnesses; a worst-axis cap keeps the Controller's contract to one enum. *Rejected.* One scalar "health" (a hidden stat, P1); band-entry-only states (kills inside the Graph's DOOR-BREACH window with no tell). *Criterion.* AC-SE-18: a cap-mapping table test; the Controller source references no other Suit field. *Pillar.* P1, P2.

**SE-17. An axis climbs one rung per qualifying hold; descent is never rate-limited (`vitals_ladder_step`).** *Rule.* For the two **counter axes** (breath, integrity) each run evaluates a **demand** `d` in 0..2 and a **terminal condition** `t` from the true state (SE-19, SE-22). With `r` the current rung: (1) if `r ≤ 2` and `d < r`, the rung drops to `d` **immediately** (any distance) and the hold resets; (2) if `r = 0` and `d ≥ 1`, the rung becomes 1 **immediately** — the first cue fires as early as the true state allows; (3) if `r = 1` and `d ≥ 2`, a hold counter increments and the rung becomes 2 when it reaches `HOLD_12 = ceil(RUNG_MIN_TICKS / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` runs (100 runs = 10 s at `RUNG_MIN_TICKS` **600**); (4) if `r = 2` and `t`, a hold counter increments and the rung becomes 3 when it reaches `HOLD_23(axis)` runs (breath: `max(HOLD_12, HYPOX_FAIL_S × (TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS))`, 150 runs = 15 s at `HYPOX_FAIL_S` 15 `[PH]`; integrity: `max(HOLD_12, INTEG_FAIL_S × (TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS))`, 100 runs at `INTEG_FAIL_S` 10 `[PH]`; warmth: `(TL_MAX − TL_CRITICAL) / TL_GAIN_MAX`, 200 runs, SE-20); (5) if `r = 3`, it stays 3 while `t` holds and drops to `min(d, 2)` on the run `t` clears; (6) every promotion, demotion and every run in which the qualifying condition does not hold resets the hold to 0 — **a blip resets the counter**, so a two-second DECOMP transient never climbs past STRAINED. The **warmth axis** uses the same rung semantics but its hold is the thermal accumulator itself (SE-20) — one hold mechanism per axis, never two. *Build assertions:* `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S ≥ W_MIN_WARNING_S` (the registry's 10 s); `RUNG_MIN_TICKS ≥ SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE / 2` (600 at the defaults); `HOLD_12 + HOLD_23(axis) ≥ SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S × (TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` for each axis; for warmth, `TL_CRITICAL / TL_GAIN_MAX ≥ HOLD_12` and `HOLD_23(warmth) = (TL_MAX − TL_CRITICAL) / TL_GAIN_MAX ≥ HOLD_12`. *Both bounds* (registered as the formula `vitals_ladder_step`, D.3): the **floor** is that the earliest FAILED after the first cue (entry to STRAINED) is `HOLD_12 + HOLD_23` runs; the **cap** is that a demand which holds for fewer than a hold's length never advances — both bounds hold for endogenous demand traces; the integrity axis under `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` 1 is excluded from the cap bound and tested by AC-SE-24. *(orchestrator ruling: the stage ruling's first arithmetic — a divisor of 3 giving 200 ticks — undercounts, because the first cue fires on entering STRAINED and so only two rate-limited advances separate it from FAILED; the divisor is 2 and the default is 600 ticks.)* *Residual risk RK-3 (breath-hold exploit):* a player oscillating between a vacuum and a breathable compartment resets the counters each time and so could extend reserve-0 time; the physical feasibility is low (a room change needs a door), there is no decay branch (the hold resets in full on any non-qualifying run), and the trade-off is stated here rather than silently coded. *Why.* A rate-limited ascent with instant descent is what makes the tell floor hold when the Graph's input jumps and what makes the Controller's "cap first, then death" check at `death_tick` meaningful (a rescued seat is not kept DOWNED for twenty seconds). *Rejected.* Symmetric rate limiting; a leaky-bucket reset as the default. *Criterion.* AC-SE-19 (endogenous fixtures and Suit-owned damage paths only): property test over random target traces (at most +1 rung per hold; a descent is instant; on a counter axis the stagger edge FULL→WALK_ONLY recurs no more often than once per `HOLD_12` runs, and on the warmth axis the same bound is bought by the re-entry refractory of D.4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Pillar.* P2.

**SE-18. The tell floor.** *Rule.* `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S` **20 s** `[PH]` (range 10-30; build assertion ≥ `W_MIN_WARNING_S`). The first cue is the entry to STRAINED on any axis. For every Graph fixture of the survival clocks (DOOR-BREACH, O2-ROOM, O2-SHIP, the cold fixtures) and every Suit-owned damage path, the elapsed ticks from `vitals_state` first leaving OK to FAILED is ≥ `SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE`. This is Suit's own Pillar 2 floor because the Graph's fairness gate covers endogenous *ship* transitions only and says outright that "Player death is Suit's rule" (LS-13); the Graph guarantees that the *cause* was warned. Ambient band cues that precede STRAINED (breath vapour in COOL air) are not counted — the measure is conservative. For the integrity axis the first body cue is the seam jet, not the STRAINED entry (SE-22, CD-GDD-ALIGN 2026-10-26), and the floor is met measured from the jet too (A3 with A7). The floor is a property of the *endogenous* paths; an exogenous source (a Horror act, friendly harm) carries its own 10-30 s tell (the Graph's exported requirement) and reaches this ladder like any other source (SE-23). *Criterion.* AC-SE-20 (endogenous fixtures and Suit-owned damage paths only): the death-fairness sweep over the Graph fixtures; exogenous callers are held to AC-SE-41 (review). *Pillar.* P2.

**SE-19. The breath axis.** *Rule.* Demand `d` and terminal `t` from the SE-4 source, evaluated on the reserve as it stands after this run's debit: **ROOM** — `d` 0 when `band_o2` NORMAL and `band_p` NORMAL, `d` 1 when `band_o2` THIN or `band_p` LOW *(departure from the stage ruling, logged: `band_p` LOW is added to the STRAINED demand so the DOOR-BREACH pressure fall has a Suit cue even where a vent keeps `p_o2` up; it nearly always co-occurs with THIN, so the effect is small)*; **RESERVE** — `d` 1 while `reserve_mgu` is above `RESERVE_WARN_MGU`, `d` 2 at or below it; **GASP** — `d` 2, `t` false; **NONE** — `d` 2, `t` true; the one-run flag `breath_starved` (SE-5) raises `d` to 2 for that run. The terminal condition `t` is true only for source NONE, so the breath axis can reach FAILED only in ANOXIC, DECOMP or VACUUM air with an empty reserve. *Build assertions:* A5 of D.8 — `RESERVE_WARN_S × R_BREATH ≥ (HOLD_12 × GRAPH_STEP_TICKS / TICK_RATE + W_MIN_WARNING_S) × (R_BREATH + LEAK_MGU_PER_RUN)` (20 s of engaged breath at the defaults, the leak included, so the CRITICAL cue, which arrives one hold after the threshold, still has at least `W_MIN_WARNING_S` of reserve even on a leaking suit) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* and `RESERVE_WARN_S ≤ RESERVE_S / 2`. *Why.* The ladder is keyed off enums and the reserve — never raw values or literals. *Criterion.* AC-SE-21 (the breath rung table of States and Transitions, fixture by fixture). *Pillar.* P1, P2.

**SE-20. The warmth axis and the thermal accumulator.** *Rule.* A signed integer **`thermal_load`** per seat (cold negative, heat positive, `|thermal_load| ≤ TL_MAX`) integrates the true temperature excess and relaxes in mild air. **There is no readable body-temperature number** — it is a hidden-stat reading and the first target a hallucination would take; the witnesses are the sprite (shiver, rime), breath vapour, the audio and the room itself. Per run: *gain* happens only while the compartment's raw `T` is **past the effective onset** — cold: below `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` minus the effective insulation (SE-21); heat: above `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` — and its size grows with depth past the onset between `TL_GAIN_MIN` and `TL_GAIN_MAX` per run (saturating at `TL_DEPTH_CAP_MC`); *relax* happens when there is no gain: at `TL_RELAX_COMFORT` in COMFORT, `TL_RELAX_COOL` in COOL, `TL_RELAX_EDGE` in FROST, HOT or FREEZING (the last only when there is no gain, i.e. above the shifted onset), and not at all in DEEP_COLD or SCORCH *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **Re-entry refractory:** after the warmth rung falls from 2 or more to below 2, it may not climb from 1 to 2 for `HOLD_12` runs (`rf_warmth`, D.4); first entries are never delayed. The rungs: **STRAINED** when `band_t` is FROST, FREEZING, DEEP_COLD, HOT or SCORCH (so the first cue appears with the band, before any gain), or `|thermal_load| ≥ TL_STRAINED` (a lingering warmth that eases only as the load relaxes); **CRITICAL** when `|thermal_load| ≥ TL_CRITICAL` (descending below `TL_CRITICAL − TL_HYST`); **FAILED** when `|thermal_load| ≥ TL_MAX` (descending below `TL_MAX − TL_HYST`); ascent at most one rung per run. FROST, FREEZING above the shifted onset (from the FREEZING boundary down to `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC − insul_eff_mc`) and HOT below `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` saturate below CRITICAL: they hold a seat at STRAINED (shiver, rime); from a fresh start none of them can reach CRITICAL, so none can walk-lock or kill, and because all three relax a seat that arrives already at CRITICAL recovers from it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; only cold past the shifted onset, heat past `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC`, SCORCH or DEEP_COLD can. `HOLD_23(warmth)` is a **derived output**, `(TL_MAX − TL_CRITICAL) / TL_GAIN_MAX` runs (200 runs at the defaults), and `THERMAL_FAIL_S` is that value in seconds (20 s), not a free knob; the build assertions of SE-17 hold it at or above `HOLD_12`. Warmth is read from the raw `T` against the four registry names only (no hysteresis on the gain, which the integrator smooths; the rung descent carries `TL_HYST`). *(orchestrator ruling: one hold mechanism. The game-designer proposal ran a thermal integrator and a separate sustained-hold counter, which the stage ruling forbids ("one hold mechanism, not two"); the systems-designer's resolution is adopted — for the warmth axis `thermal_load` IS the hold accumulator, rungs follow its thresholds, and the tell floor is guaranteed arithmetically by the SE-17 build assertions.
